```
     1→// Package id provides the CheckpointID type for identifying checkpoints.
     2→// This is a separate package to avoid import cycles between paths, trailers, and checkpoint.
     3→package id
     4→
     5→import (
     6→	"crypto/rand"
     7→	"encoding/hex"
     8→	"fmt"
     9→	"regexp"
    10→)
    11→
    12→// CheckpointID is a 12-character hex identifier for checkpoints.
    13→// It's used to link code commits to metadata on the entire/sessions branch.
    14→type CheckpointID string
    15→
    16→// EmptyCheckpointID represents an unset or invalid checkpoint ID.
    17→const EmptyCheckpointID CheckpointID = ""
    18→
    19→// Pattern is the regex pattern for a valid checkpoint ID: exactly 12 lowercase hex characters.
    20→// Exported for use in other packages (e.g., trailers) to avoid pattern duplication.
    21→const Pattern = `[0-9a-f]{12}`
    22→
    23→// checkpointIDRegex validates the format: exactly 12 lowercase hex characters.
    24→var checkpointIDRegex = regexp.MustCompile(`^` + Pattern + `$`)
    25→
    26→// NewCheckpointID creates a CheckpointID from a string, validating its format.
    27→// Returns an error if the string is not a valid 12-character hex ID.
    28→func NewCheckpointID(s string) (CheckpointID, error) {
    29→	if err := Validate(s); err != nil {
    30→		return EmptyCheckpointID, err
    31→	}
    32→	return CheckpointID(s), nil
    33→}
    34→
    35→// MustCheckpointID creates a CheckpointID from a string, panicking if invalid.
    36→// Use only when the ID is known to be valid (e.g., from trusted sources).
    37→func MustCheckpointID(s string) CheckpointID {
    38→	id, err := NewCheckpointID(s)
    39→	if err != nil {
    40→		panic(err)
    41→	}
    42→	return id
    43→}
    44→
    45→// Generate creates a new random 12-character hex checkpoint ID.
    46→func Generate() CheckpointID {
    47→	bytes := make([]byte, 6) // 6 bytes = 12 hex chars
    48→	if _, err := rand.Read(bytes); err != nil {
    49→		panic(fmt.Sprintf("failed to generate random checkpoint ID: %v", err))
    50→	}
    51→	return CheckpointID(hex.EncodeToString(bytes))
    52→}
    53→
    54→// Validate checks if a string is a valid checkpoint ID format.
    55→// Returns an error if invalid, nil if valid.
    56→func Validate(s string) error {
    57→	if !checkpointIDRegex.MatchString(s) {
    58→		return fmt.Errorf("invalid checkpoint ID %q: must be 12 lowercase hex characters", s)
    59→	}
    60→	return nil
    61→}
    62→
    63→// String returns the checkpoint ID as a string.
    64→func (id CheckpointID) String() string {
    65→	return string(id)
    66→}
    67→
    68→// IsEmpty returns true if the checkpoint ID is empty or unset.
    69→func (id CheckpointID) IsEmpty() bool {
    70→	return id == EmptyCheckpointID
    71→}
    72→
    73→// Path returns the sharded path for this checkpoint ID on entire/sessions.
    74→// Uses first 2 characters as shard (256 buckets), remaining as folder name.
    75→// Example: "a3b2c4d5e6f7" -> "a3/b2c4d5e6f7"
    76→func (id CheckpointID) Path() string {
    77→	if len(id) < 3 {
    78→		return string(id)
    79→	}
    80→	return string(id[:2]) + "/" + string(id[2:])
    81→}
    82→
```

```
     1→// Package id provides the CheckpointID type for identifying checkpoints.
     2→// This is a separate package to avoid import cycles between paths, trailers, and checkpoint.
     3→package id
     4→
     5→import (
     6→	"crypto/rand"
     7→	"encoding/hex"
     8→	"fmt"
     9→	"regexp"
    10→)
    11→
    12→// CheckpointID is a 12-character hex identifier for checkpoints.
    13→// It's used to link code commits to metadata on the entire/sessions branch.
    14→type CheckpointID string
    15→
    16→// EmptyCheckpointID represents an unset or invalid checkpoint ID.
    17→const EmptyCheckpointID CheckpointID = ""
    18→
    19→// Pattern is the regex pattern for a valid checkpoint ID: exactly 12 lowercase hex characters.
    20→// Exported for use in other packages (e.g., trailers) to avoid pattern duplication.
    21→const Pattern = `[0-9a-f]{12}`
    22→
    23→// checkpointIDRegex validates the format: exactly 12 lowercase hex characters.
    24→var checkpointIDRegex = regexp.MustCompile(`^` + Pattern + `$`)
    25→
    26→// NewCheckpointID creates a CheckpointID from a string, validating its format.
    27→// Returns an error if the string is not a valid 12-character hex ID.
    28→func NewCheckpointID(s string) (CheckpointID, error) {
    29→	if err := Validate(s); err != nil {
    30→		return EmptyCheckpointID, err
    31→	}
    32→	return CheckpointID(s), nil
    33→}
    34→
    35→// MustCheckpointID creates a CheckpointID from a string, panicking if invalid.
    36→// Use only when the ID is known to be valid (e.g., from trusted sources).
    37→func MustCheckpointID(s string) CheckpointID {
    38→	id, err := NewCheckpointID(s)
    39→	if err != nil {
    40→		panic(err)
    41→	}
    42→	return id
    43→}
    44→
    45→// Generate creates a new random 12-character hex checkpoint ID.
    46→func Generate() (CheckpointID, error) {
    47→	bytes := make([]byte, 6) // 6 bytes = 12 hex chars
    48→	if _, err := rand.Read(bytes); err != nil {
    49→		return EmptyCheckpointID, fmt.Errorf("failed to generate random checkpoint ID: %w", err)
    50→	}
    51→	return CheckpointID(hex.EncodeToString(bytes)), nil
    52→}
    53→
    54→// Validate checks if a string is a valid checkpoint ID format.
    55→// Returns an error if invalid, nil if valid.
    56→func Validate(s string) error {
    57→	if !checkpointIDRegex.MatchString(s) {
    58→		return fmt.Errorf("invalid checkpoint ID %q: must be 12 lowercase hex characters", s)
    59→	}
    60→	return nil
    61→}
    62→
    63→// String returns the checkpoint ID as a string.
    64→func (id CheckpointID) String() string {
    65→	return string(id)
    66→}
    67→
    68→// IsEmpty returns true if the checkpoint ID is empty or unset.
    69→func (id CheckpointID) IsEmpty() bool {
    70→	return id == EmptyCheckpointID
    71→}
    72→
    73→// Path returns the sharded path for this checkpoint ID on entire/sessions.
    74→// Uses first 2 characters as shard (256 buckets), remaining as folder name.
    75→// Example: "a3b2c4d5e6f7" -> "a3/b2c4d5e6f7"
    76→func (id CheckpointID) Path() string {
    77→	if len(id) < 3 {
    78→		return string(id)
    79→	}
    80→	return string(id[:2]) + "/" + string(id[2:])
    81→}
    82→
```

> AGENT

Done. Replaced all direct `id.CheckpointID(string)` casts with `id.MustCheckpointID()` to make the "trusted source" assumption explicit:

**Changes made:**

1. `cmd/entire/cli/paths/paths.go:45-48` - `CheckpointPath()` now uses `MustCheckpointID()` with documentation noting it panics on invalid IDs

2. `cmd/entire/cli/strategy/manual_commit_rewind.go:227` - Uses `MustCheckpointID()` for JSON-sourced checkpoint ID

3. `cmd/entire/cli/strategy/session.go:115` - Uses `MustCheckpointID()` for internal checkpoint listing data

4. `cmd/entire/cli/resume.go:401` - Uses `MustCheckpointID()` for validated checkpoint from lookup

**Test fixes:**

5. `cmd/entire/cli/strategy/session_test.go:359` - Changed "multi12345678" (13 chars) to valid "ab12cd34ef56"

6. `cmd/entire/cli/resume_test.go:563` - Changed "different12345" (14 chars) to valid "abcd12345678"

The API now provides clear choices:
- `NewCheckpointID(s)` - Returns error for invalid IDs (use for untrusted input)
- `MustCheckpointID(s)` - Panics on invalid IDs (use for trusted sources like our own stored data)
- `Generate()` - Creates new random IDs (returns error tuple)

> DEVELOPER

hmm but panics in code are not great either, any other option?

> AGENT

You're right. Let me propose better alternatives:

**Option 1: Return errors and handle gracefully**
Change call sites to use `NewCheckpointID()` and handle errors (skip invalid entries with logging rather than crashing).

**Option 2: Type-safe structs with JSON validation**
Add JSON marshal/unmarshal to `CheckpointID` type, then change struct fields from `string` to `CheckpointID`. Validation happens once at deserialization with proper error handling.

**Option 3: Hybrid - `CheckpointPath` returns error, internal data uses typed fields**
`CheckpointPath(string) (string, error)` for the utility function, and gradually migrate structs to use `CheckpointID` type.

Option 2 is cleanest long-term but bigger refactor. Option 1 is simplest immediate fix.

Which approach do you prefer?

> DEVELOPER

let's try 2=

> AGENT

I'll add JSON marshaling to `CheckpointID` and migrate the structs to use the typed field.

```
     1→// Package id provides the CheckpointID type for identifying checkpoints.
     2→// This is a separate package to avoid import cycles between paths, trailers, and checkpoint.
     3→package id
     4→
     5→import (
     6→	"crypto/rand"
     7→	"encoding/hex"
     8→	"fmt"
     9→	"regexp"
    10→)
    11→
    12→// CheckpointID is a 12-character hex identifier for checkpoints.
    13→// It's used to link code commits to metadata on the entire/sessions branch.
    14→type CheckpointID string
    15→
    16→// EmptyCheckpointID represents an unset or invalid checkpoint ID.
    17→const EmptyCheckpointID CheckpointID = ""
    18→
    19→// Pattern is the regex pattern for a valid checkpoint ID: exactly 12 lowercase hex characters.
    20→// Exported for use in other packages (e.g., trailers) to avoid pattern duplication.
    21→const Pattern = `[0-9a-f]{12}`
    22→
    23→// checkpointIDRegex validates the format: exactly 12 lowercase hex characters.
    24→var checkpointIDRegex = regexp.MustCompile(`^` + Pattern + `$`)
    25→
    26→// NewCheckpointID creates a CheckpointID from a string, validating its format.
    27→// Returns an error if the string is not a valid 12-character hex ID.
    28→func NewCheckpointID(s string) (CheckpointID, error) {
    29→	if err := Validate(s); err != nil {
    30→		return EmptyCheckpointID, err
    31→	}
    32→	return CheckpointID(s), nil
    33→}
    34→
    35→// MustCheckpointID creates a CheckpointID from a string, panicking if invalid.
    36→// Use only when the ID is known to be valid (e.g., from trusted sources).
    37→func MustCheckpointID(s string) CheckpointID {
    38→	id, err := NewCheckpointID(s)
    39→	if err != nil {
    40→		panic(err)
    41→	}
    42→	return id
    43→}
    44→
    45→// Generate creates a new random 12-character hex checkpoint ID.
    46→func Generate() (CheckpointID, error) {
    47→	bytes := make([]byte, 6) // 6 bytes = 12 hex chars
    48→	if _, err := rand.Read(bytes); err != nil {
    49→		return EmptyCheckpointID, fmt.Errorf("failed to generate random checkpoint ID: %w", err)
    50→	}
    51→	return CheckpointID(hex.EncodeToString(bytes)), nil
    52→}
    53→
    54→// Validate checks if a string is a valid checkpoint ID format.
    55→// Returns an error if invalid, nil if valid.
    56→func Validate(s string) error {
    57→	if !checkpointIDRegex.MatchString(s) {
    58→		return fmt.Errorf("invalid checkpoint ID %q: must be 12 lowercase hex characters", s)
    59→	}
    60→	return nil
    61→}
    62→
    63→// String returns the checkpoint ID as a string.
    64→func (id CheckpointID) String() string {
    65→	return string(id)
    66→}
    67→
    68→// IsEmpty returns true if the checkpoint ID is empty or unset.
    69→func (id CheckpointID) IsEmpty() bool {
    70→	return id == EmptyCheckpointID
    71→}
    72→
    73→// Path returns the sharded path for this checkpoint ID on entire/sessions.
    74→// Uses first 2 characters as shard (256 buckets), remaining as folder name.
    75→// Example: "a3b2c4d5e6f7" -> "a3/b2c4d5e6f7"
    76→func (id CheckpointID) Path() string {
    77→	if len(id) < 3 {
    78→		return string(id)
    79→	}
    80→	return string(id[:2]) + "/" + string(id[2:])
    81→}
    82→
```

```json
  "old_string": "// Path returns the sharded path for this checkpoint ID on entire/sessions.\n// Uses first 2 characters as shard (256 buckets), remaining as folder name.\n// Example: \"a3b2c4d5e6f7\" -> \"a3/b2c4d5e6f7\"\nfunc (id CheckpointID) Path() string {\n\tif len(id) < 3 {\n\t\treturn string(id)\n\t}\n\treturn string(id[:2]) + \"/\" + string(id[2:])\n}",
  "new_string": "// Path returns the sharded path for this checkpoint ID on entire/sessions.\n// Uses first 2 characters as shard (256 buckets), remaining as folder name.\n// Example: \"a3b2c4d5e6f7\" -> \"a3/b2c4d5e6f7\"\nfunc (id CheckpointID) Path() string {\n\tif len(id) < 3 {\n\t\treturn string(id)\n\t}\n\treturn string(id[:2]) + \"/\" + string(id[2:])\n}\n\n// MarshalJSON implements json.Marshaler.\nfunc (id CheckpointID) MarshalJSON() ([]byte, error) {\n\treturn json.Marshal(string(id))\n}\n\n// UnmarshalJSON implements json.Unmarshaler with validation.\n// Returns an error if the JSON string is not a valid 12-character hex ID.\n// Empty strings are allowed and result in EmptyCheckpointID.\nfunc (id *CheckpointID) UnmarshalJSON(data []byte) error {\n\tvar s string\n\tif err := json.Unmarshal(data, &s); err != nil {\n\t\treturn err\n\t}\n\t// Allow empty strings (represents unset checkpoint ID)\n\tif s == \"\" {\n\t\t*id = EmptyCheckpointID\n\t\treturn nil\n\t}\n\tif err := Validate(s); err != nil {\n\t\treturn err\n\t}\n\t*id = CheckpointID(s)\n\treturn nil\n}"
}
```

```
    25→type State struct {
    26→	// SessionID is the unique session identifier
    27→	SessionID string `json:"session_id"`
    28→
    29→	// BaseCommit is the HEAD commit when the session started
    30→	BaseCommit string `json:"base_commit"`
    31→

    33→	WorktreePath string `json:"worktree_path,omitempty"`
    34→
    35→	// StartedAt is when the session was started
    36→	StartedAt time.Time `json:"started_at"`
    37→
    38→	// CheckpointCount is the number of checkpoints created in this session
    39→	CheckpointCount int `json:"checkpoint_count"`
    40→
    41→	// CondensedTranscriptLines tracks lines already included in previous condensation
    42→	CondensedTranscriptLines int `json:"condensed_transcript_lines,omitempty"`
    43→
    44→	// UntrackedFilesAtStart tracks files that existed at session start (to preserve during rewind)
    45→	UntrackedFilesAtStart []string `json:"untracked_files_at_start,omitempty"`
    46→
    47→	// FilesTouched tracks files modified/created/deleted during this session
    48→	FilesTouched []string `json:"files_touched,omitempty"`
    49→
    50→	// ConcurrentWarningShown is true if user was warned about concurrent sessions
    51→	ConcurrentWarningShown bool `json:"concurrent_warning_shown,omitempty"`
    52→
    53→	// LastCheckpointID is the checkpoint ID from last condensation, reused for subsequent commits without new content
    54→	LastCheckpointID string `json:"last_checkpoint_id,omitempty"`
    55→
    56→	// AgentType identifies the agent that created this session (e.g., "Claude Code", "Gemini CLI", "Cursor")
    57→	AgentType string `json:"agent_type,omitempty"`
    58→
    59→	// Token usage tracking (accumulated across all checkpoints in this session)

    61→
    62→	// Transcript position when session started (for multi-session checkpoints)
    63→	TranscriptLinesAtStart int    `json:"transcript_lines_at_start,omitempty"`
    64→	TranscriptUUIDAtStart  string `json:"transcript_uuid_at_start,omitempty"`
```

```
   691→	}

   694→	branchName := GetCurrentBranchName(repo)
```

```
    99→	// Get shadow branch
```

```
   102→	ref, err := repo.Reference(refName, true)

   105→	}
   106→
   107→	// Extract session data, starting from where we left off last condensation
```

```
   406→		// This prevents stale BaseCommit when commits happen without condensation
   407→		// (e.g., when reusing a previous checkpoint ID for split commits)
   408→		newHead := head.Hash().String()
   409→		for _, state := range sessions {
   410→			if state.BaseCommit != newHead {
   411→				state.BaseCommit = newHead
   412→				if err := s.saveSessionState(state); err != nil {
   413→					logging.Warn(logCtx, "post-commit: failed to update session BaseCommit",

   423→	// Track shadow branches to clean up after successful condensation

   437→
   438→		// Update session state for the new base commit
   439→		// After condensation, the session continues from the NEW commit (HEAD), so we:

   441→		// 2. Reset CheckpointCount to 0 - no checkpoints exist on new shadow branch yet
```

```
   324→// checkRemoteMetadata checks if checkpoint metadata exists on origin/entire/sessions
   325→// and automatically fetches it if available.
   326→func checkRemoteMetadata(repo *git.Repository, checkpointID string) error {
   327→	// Try to get remote metadata branch tree
   328→	remoteTree, err := strategy.GetRemoteMetadataBranchTree(repo)
   329→	if err != nil {
   330→		fmt.Fprintf(os.Stderr, "Checkpoint '%s' found in commit but session metadata not available\n", checkpointID)
   331→		fmt.Fprintf(os.Stderr, "The entire/sessions branch may not exist locally or on the remote.\n")
   332→		return nil //nolint:nilerr // Informational message, not a fatal error
   333→	}
```

```json
  "old_string": "// checkRemoteMetadata checks if checkpoint metadata exists on origin/entire/sessions\n// and automatically fetches it if available.\nfunc checkRemoteMetadata(repo *git.Repository, checkpointID string) error {\n\t// Try to get remote metadata branch tree\n\tremoteTree, err := strategy.GetRemoteMetadataBranchTree(repo)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\tfmt.Fprintf(os.Stderr, \"Checkpoint '%s' found in commit but session metadata not available\\n\", checkpointID)\n\t\tfmt.Fprintf(os.Stderr, \"The entire/sessions branch may not exist locally or on the remote.\\n\")\n\t\treturn nil //nolint:nilerr // Informational message, not a fatal error\n\t}\n\n\t// Check if the checkpoint exists on the remote\n\tmetadata, err := strategy.ReadCheckpointMetadata(remoteTree, paths.CheckpointPath(checkpointID))\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\tfmt.Fprintf(os.Stderr, \"Checkpoint '%s' found in commit but session metadata not available\\n\", checkpointID)\n\t\treturn nil //nolint:nilerr // Informational message, not a fatal error\n\t}\n\n\t// Metadata exists on remote but not locally - fetch it automatically\n\tfmt.Fprintf(os.Stderr, \"Fetching session metadata from origin...\\n\")\n\tif err := FetchMetadataBranch(); err != nil {\n\t\tfmt.Fprintf(os.Stderr, \"Failed to fetch metadata: %v\\n\", err)\n\t\tfmt.Fprintf(os.Stderr, \"You can try manually: git fetch origin entire/sessions:entire/sessions\\n\")\n\t\treturn NewSilentError(errors.New(\"failed to fetch metadata\"))\n\t}\n\n\t// Now resume the session with the fetched metadata\n\treturn resumeSession(metadata.SessionID, checkpointID, false)\n}",
  "new_string": "// checkRemoteMetadata checks if checkpoint metadata exists on origin/entire/sessions\n// and automatically fetches it if available.\nfunc checkRemoteMetadata(repo *git.Repository, checkpointID id.CheckpointID) error {\n\t// Try to get remote metadata branch tree\n\tremoteTree, err := strategy.GetRemoteMetadataBranchTree(repo)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\tfmt.Fprintf(os.Stderr, \"Checkpoint '%s' found in commit but session metadata not available\\n\", checkpointID)\n\t\tfmt.Fprintf(os.Stderr, \"The entire/sessions branch may not exist locally or on the remote.\\n\")\n\t\treturn nil //nolint:nilerr // Informational message, not a fatal error\n\t}\n\n\t// Check if the checkpoint exists on the remote\n\tmetadata, err := strategy.ReadCheckpointMetadata(remoteTree, checkpointID.Path())\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\tfmt.Fprintf(os.Stderr, \"Checkpoint '%s' found in commit but session metadata not available\\n\", checkpointID)\n\t\treturn nil //nolint:nilerr // Informational message, not a fatal error\n\t}\n\n\t// Metadata exists on remote but not locally - fetch it automatically\n\tfmt.Fprintf(os.Stderr, \"Fetching session metadata from origin...\\n\")\n\tif err := FetchMetadataBranch(); err != nil {\n\t\tfmt.Fprintf(os.Stderr, \"Failed to fetch metadata: %v\\n\", err)\n\t\tfmt.Fprintf(os.Stderr, \"You can try manually: git fetch origin entire/sessions:entire/sessions\\n\")\n\t\treturn NewSilentError(errors.New(\"failed to fetch metadata\"))\n\t}\n\n\t// Now resume the session with the fetched metadata\n\treturn resumeSession(metadata.SessionID, checkpointID.String(), false)\n}"
}
```

```
   312→	checkpointID := "abc123def456" // Fixed ID for testing

   314→	// Get existing metadata branch or create it

   337→
   338→	// Create blob for metadata

   349→		t.Fatalf("Failed to close writer: %v", err)
   350→	}
   351→	metadataBlobHash, err := repo.Storer.SetEncodedObject(blob)
   352→	if err != nil {

   354→	}
   355→
   356→	// Create session log blob

   366→	if err := logWriter.Close(); err != nil {

   368→	}
   369→	logBlobHash, err := repo.Storer.SetEncodedObject(logBlob)

   372→	}
   373→
   374→	// Build tree structure: <id[:2]>/<id[2:]>/metadata.json
   375→	shardedPath := paths.CheckpointPath(checkpointID)
   376→
   377→	// Create checkpoint tree with metadata and transcript files
   378→	// Entries must be sorted alphabetically

   388→	}

   393→
```

```
   397→// Helper function to create a test metadata branch with a multi-session checkpoint
   398→func createTestMultiSessionCheckpoint(t *testing.T, repo *git.Repository, checkpointID, primarySessionID string, allSessionIDs []string) {
   399→	t.Helper()
   400→
   401→	entries := make(map[string]object.TreeEntry)
   402→	checkpointPath := paths.CheckpointPath(checkpointID)
   403→
   404→	// Create metadata.json with SessionIDs array
   405→	metadata := CheckpointInfo{
   406→		CheckpointID: checkpointID,
   407→		SessionID:    primarySessionID,
   408→		SessionCount: len(allSessionIDs),
   409→		SessionIDs:   allSessionIDs,
   410→		CreatedAt:    time.Now(),
   411→	}
   412→	metadataJSON, err := json.Marshal(metadata)
   413→	if err != nil {
   414→		t.Fatalf("failed to marshal metadata: %v", err)
   415→	}
   416→	metadataBlobHash, err := checkpoint.CreateBlobFromContent(repo, metadataJSON)
   417→	if err != nil {
   418→		t.Fatalf("failed to create metadata blob: %v", err)
   419→	}
   420→	entries[checkpointPath+"/"+paths.MetadataFileName] = object.TreeEntry{
   421→		Name: checkpointPath + "/" + paths.MetadataFileName,
   422→		Mode: filemode.Regular,
   423→		Hash: metadataBlobHash,
   424→	}
   425→
   426→	// Build tree
   427→	treeHash, err := checkpoint.BuildTreeFromEntries(repo, entries)
   428→	if err != nil {
   429→		t.Fatalf("failed to build tree: %v", err)
   430→	}
   431→
   432→	// Create orphan commit
   433→	now := time.Now()
   434→	sig := object.Signature{
   435→		Name:  "Test",
   436→		Email: "test@test.com",
   437→		When:  now,
   438→	}
   439→	commit := &object.Commit{
   440→		TreeHash:  treeHash,
   441→		Author:    sig,
   442→		Committer: sig,
   443→		Message:   "Multi-session checkpoint\n\n" + trailers.SessionTrailerKey + ": " + primarySessionID,
   444→	}
   445→
   446→	commitObj := repo.Storer.NewEncodedObject()
   447→	if err := commit.Encode(commitObj); err != nil {
   448→		t.Fatalf("failed to encode commit: %v", err)
   449→	}
   450→	commitHash, err := repo.Storer.SetEncodedObject(commitObj)
   451→	if err != nil {
   452→		t.Fatalf("failed to store commit: %v", err)
   453→	}
   454→
   455→	// Create branch reference
   456→	refName := plumbing.NewBranchReferenceName(paths.MetadataBranchName)
   457→	ref := plumbing.NewHashReference(refName, commitHash)
   458→	if err := repo.Storer.SetReference(ref); err != nil {
   459→		t.Fatalf("failed to create branch: %v", err)
   460→	}
   461→}
   462→
```

```
     1→// Package id provides the CheckpointID type for identifying checkpoints.
     2→// This is a separate package to avoid import cycles between paths, trailers, and checkpoint.
     3→package id
     4→
     5→import (
     6→	"crypto/rand"
     7→	"encoding/hex"
     8→	"encoding/json"
     9→	"fmt"
    10→	"regexp"
    11→)
    12→
    13→// CheckpointID is a 12-character hex identifier for checkpoints.
    14→// It's used to link code commits to metadata on the entire/sessions branch.
    15→type CheckpointID string
    16→
    17→// EmptyCheckpointID represents an unset or invalid checkpoint ID.
    18→const EmptyCheckpointID CheckpointID = ""
    19→
    20→// Pattern is the regex pattern for a valid checkpoint ID: exactly 12 lowercase hex characters.
    21→// Exported for use in other packages (e.g., trailers) to avoid pattern duplication.
    22→const Pattern = `[0-9a-f]{12}`
    23→
    24→// checkpointIDRegex validates the format: exactly 12 lowercase hex characters.
    25→var checkpointIDRegex = regexp.MustCompile(`^` + Pattern + `$`)
    26→
    27→// NewCheckpointID creates a CheckpointID from a string, validating its format.
    28→// Returns an error if the string is not a valid 12-character hex ID.
    29→func NewCheckpointID(s string) (CheckpointID, error) {
    30→	if err := Validate(s); err != nil {
    31→		return EmptyCheckpointID, err
    32→	}
    33→	return CheckpointID(s), nil
    34→}
    35→
    36→// MustCheckpointID creates a CheckpointID from a string, panicking if invalid.
    37→// Use only when the ID is known to be valid (e.g., from trusted sources).
    38→func MustCheckpointID(s string) CheckpointID {
    39→	id, err := NewCheckpointID(s)
    40→	if err != nil {
    41→		panic(err)
    42→	}
    43→	return id
    44→}
    45→
    46→// Generate creates a new random 12-character hex checkpoint ID.
    47→func Generate() (CheckpointID, error) {
    48→	bytes := make([]byte, 6) // 6 bytes = 12 hex chars
    49→	if _, err := rand.Read(bytes); err != nil {
    50→		return EmptyCheckpointID, fmt.Errorf("failed to generate random checkpoint ID: %w", err)
    51→	}
    52→	return CheckpointID(hex.EncodeToString(bytes)), nil
    53→}
    54→
    55→// Validate checks if a string is a valid checkpoint ID format.
    56→// Returns an error if invalid, nil if valid.
    57→func Validate(s string) error {
    58→	if !checkpointIDRegex.MatchString(s) {
    59→		return fmt.Errorf("invalid checkpoint ID %q: must be 12 lowercase hex characters", s)
    60→	}
    61→	return nil
    62→}
    63→
    64→// String returns the checkpoint ID as a string.
    65→func (id CheckpointID) String() string {
    66→	return string(id)
    67→}
    68→
    69→// IsEmpty returns true if the checkpoint ID is empty or unset.
    70→func (id CheckpointID) IsEmpty() bool {
    71→	return id == EmptyCheckpointID
    72→}
    73→
    74→// Path returns the sharded path for this checkpoint ID on entire/sessions.
    75→// Uses first 2 characters as shard (256 buckets), remaining as folder name.
    76→// Example: "a3b2c4d5e6f7" -> "a3/b2c4d5e6f7"
    77→func (id CheckpointID) Path() string {
    78→	if len(id) < 3 {
    79→		return string(id)
    80→	}
    81→	return string(id[:2]) + "/" + string(id[2:])
    82→}
    83→

    85→func (id CheckpointID) MarshalJSON() ([]byte, error) {
```

> AGENT

The refactoring is complete. Here's a summary of what was accomplished:

**Option 2 Implementation: Type-safe CheckpointID with JSON validation**

1. **Added JSON marshal/unmarshal to `id.CheckpointID`** (`checkpoint/id/id.go`):
   - `MarshalJSON()` - serializes the ID as a JSON string
   - `UnmarshalJSON()` - deserializes and validates that the string is a valid 12-hex-char ID, rejecting invalid values at deserialization time

2. **Updated struct fields from `string` to `id.CheckpointID`**:
   - `checkpoint/checkpoint.go`: `WriteCommittedOptions.CheckpointID`, `CommittedInfo.CheckpointID`, `CommittedMetadata.CheckpointID`
   - `strategy/manual_commit_types.go`: `SessionState.LastCheckpointID`, `CheckpointInfo.CheckpointID`, `CondenseResult.CheckpointID`
   - `session/state.go`: `State.LastCheckpointID`
   - `strategy/session.go`: `Checkpoint.CheckpointID`

3. **Updated function signatures** to take/return `id.CheckpointID` instead of strings where appropriate

4. **Fixed cascading code changes** across multiple files to use the typed ID properly:
   - Use `.String()` when string is needed (logging, string concatenation)
   - Use `.Path()` for sharded paths
   - Compare typed IDs directly instead of converting to strings
   - Use `id.MustCheckpointID()` in tests for compile-time known valid values

5. **Cleaned up** deprecated `paths.CheckpointPath()` function (marked with proper deprecation comment)

Now checkpoint IDs are validated at the JSON boundary (when deserializing from stored metadata), eliminating the need for validation at every usage site. Invalid IDs will be rejected when loading persisted data, before the code can operate on them.
